# Web UI Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build every screen of the hosted service in the approved design (sign in, sign up, password reset, the workspace with a streaming conversation and a results table, the administrator's page, account deletion) and remove the NiceGUI interface completely.

**Architecture:** Next.js pages in `web/` call the Python API directly from the browser with a short-lived token. A small pure-TypeScript layer (an SSE parser, a token cache, an API client, a reducer for the streaming turn, and derivations of the conversation and the run panel) carries the rules and is unit-tested; the React components are thin and are checked in a real browser, against the real web app and API, with a scripted stand-in for the model. The last task deletes NiceGUI and rewrites the README.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16 (App Router), React 19, TypeScript, TanStack Query, react-markdown with remark-gfm, Better Auth's client, Vitest, Playwright (Chromium).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-nextjs-auth-hosted-design.md`. This is the third of three plans: (1) the Python backend, (2) the web app's server side, (3) this plan. The design is the mock the user approved ("The design looks good for now. Go, we'll improve designs later"): Literata and Schibsted Grotesk, a pale green-grey paper, one green accent, and a yellow highlighter that marks the pages the model used.

**Where this plan departs from the spec:** the spec's header (URL bar, fetch mode, Follow links, depth, Scrape, provider picker) is reshaped into a composer: Collect (with an optional "Start from" address) or Ask about the pages (read a page, optionally with the pages it links to, then ask). There is no fetch mode picker (hosted allows only the fast mode) and no provider picker (one provider is offered). The account menu holds the daily allowance meter. The invitation token moves from the query string into the link's fragment (Task 1). It also fixes a defect in plan 1 (Task 6) and lets the hosted API use a local model (Task 0).

## Global Constraints

- **Never use a live model in a test or a check.** The repository's `.env` holds a real Mistral key; do not read it, print it or run anything from a directory that has it without a placeholder `SCRAPER_API_KEY`. Browser tests use a scripted stand-in for the model server (Task 2). A manual check of the real agent uses a local model through LM Studio (for example a Ministral 3 8B build) with `SCRAPER_PROVIDER=lmstudio`, never the hosted Mistral API.
- Node 20.9 or newer (built on Node 24, npm 11). Dependencies are pinned to exact versions: the plan 2 pins, plus @tanstack/react-query 5.104.1, react-markdown 10.1.0, remark-gfm 4.0.1 and @playwright/test 1.63.0. Chromium for Playwright is installed with `npx playwright install chromium`.
- **Design.** The look is the approved mock: Literata (reading and headings) with Schibsted Grotesk (interface), loaded with `next/font/google`; a pale green-grey paper, white surfaces, ink `#14201B`, one deep green accent `#1B5A4A`, and a yellow highlighter (`--mark`) that means exactly one thing: "the model used this page". Light and dark both follow the system. Spacing and type use the tokens in `web/src/app/globals.css`. A later design pass may restyle everything, so styles live in four CSS files (`globals.css`, `workspace.css`, `conversation.css`, `admin.css`) and no component carries inline colours.
- **Words.** Plain, from the person's side of the screen, sentence case, active voice, no apologies, no em dashes anywhere. Fixed names: "Collect", "Ask about the pages", "Send", "Send note", "Stop", "Resume", "Discard this run", "New session", "Download CSV", "Download JSON". An error says what went wrong and what to do.
- **Structure.** The browser calls the Python API directly with the bearer token (a run streams for minutes). The API's address (`API_URL`) is read on the server and passed to the browser as a prop; there is no `NEXT_PUBLIC_` setting. Streaming uses `fetch` with a stream reader, because `EventSource` cannot send a header. The token is cached until one minute before it expires, and a `401` is retried once with a new token.
- **Never render page text as HTML.** The agent's answers are Markdown that can quote any web page. They are rendered by `react-markdown` with raw HTML skipped, images removed, and only `http`, `https` and `mailto` links kept (opened in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`).
- **Performance rules** (Vercel's React guidance, applied): independent awaits run together; the Markdown library loads only when there is an answer to show; state that can be worked out from other state is worked out while rendering, not copied into more state; no component is defined inside another.
- **Accessibility.** Every control has a label; dialogs are the browser's `<dialog>` (focus trapped, Escape closes); status changes use `role="status"` or `role="alert"`; keyboard focus is always visible; `prefers-reduced-motion` stops the pulsing and the row highlight; the page never scrolls sideways at 390px wide.
- **Accounts.** Everything from plan 2 holds: invitation-only, confirmed email, the 12 character minimum, the token for the API, deletion that erases the API's data first and needs the password even on a fresh session.
- **Saved data and the NiceGUI app.** Plan 3 removes NiceGUI completely (`src/scraper/app.py`, `theme.py`, the local `__main__.py`, their tests, the `nicegui` dependency and every mention in the live docs). The SQLite `SessionStore` and the SQLite checkpointer stay for now (the agent's own tests use them); removing them is a separate cleanup.
- Database tests need `WEB_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `SCRAPER_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, each a scratch database whose name contains "test". Browser tests need `E2E_DATABASE_URL` (a scratch database whose `auth` and `app` schemas are dropped) and are run only through `npm run e2e:ui`, which starts the whole stack.
- Containers: use Podman (`podman`, `podman-compose`), not Docker, unless a file already says otherwise.
- Commit messages are short imperative sentences with no attribution lines. Work on branch `nextjs_auth_hosted`; never commit to `main`.

## Review Focus

These are failure modes the spec implies that no single task's tests would otherwise hold in view, most likely first.

1. **A run that ends in a way the screen has no words for.** The connection drops in the middle of a stream; the server refuses a run before it starts (daily limit, busy, server full); the conversation outgrows the model's context window; the model's reply is empty or malformed. Expected: the person sees a plain sentence saying what happened, the text they typed is not lost, and a run that can continue offers Resume. Pinned by the reducer and derivation tests in Task 5 (`tests/workspace/live.test.ts`, `derive.test.ts`) and by the "daily limit" and "closing the page" specs in Task 8.
2. **Text from a hostile web page shown as Markdown.** An answer that quotes a page containing an image link, a script tag, a `javascript:` link or raw HTML. Expected: only formatted text appears; nothing is fetched from the quoted address; no script runs. Pinned twice: from saved history in Task 7 and from a live stream in Task 8.
3. **The same account in two tabs, a reload, or leaving the page in the middle of a run.** Expected: a second tab shows the run as working and can send it a note; a reload shows a stopped run with Resume; nothing is shown twice and nothing is lost. Pinned by the "second tab", "closing the page" and "reload" specs in Task 8.
4. **A token that expires while the page is open.** The client must fetch a new token (once) when the API answers `401`, and a stream that is already running must not be cut at the 15 minute mark, because the token is checked only when the stream starts. Pinned by `tests/api/client.test.ts` and `tests/api/token.test.ts` (Task 4); the second half is a property of the API and is not tested here.
5. **Removing a person's data.** Deleting your own account and an administrator removing someone must erase what the agent saved for them, and a wrong password must delete nothing. Pinned by the deletion specs in Task 10 and the administrator spec in Task 9, which read the `app` tables directly.
6. **Result tables the column order of which changes after a reload.** Postgres `jsonb` sorts an object's keys, so a table loaded from the database came back in a different column order from the one the agent chose. Fixed and pinned in Task 6; check any new column of JSON for the same thing.

---

### Task 0: Let the hosted API use a local model

Plan 2's end-to-end check had to give the API a Mistral address that goes nowhere. The browser tests in this plan need a real model conversation, scripted. This task makes a local model server a first-class choice for the hosted API, which is also how a developer runs the whole service without a Mistral account.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/scraper/config.py`, `src/scraper_api/app.py`, `src/scraper_api/__main__.py`, `docs/hosted-deploy.md`
- Create: `tests/test_hosted_provider.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: The hosted `Config` and `build_deps` from plan 1.
- Produces: `SCRAPER_PROVIDER=lmstudio` works on the hosted API (no key needed, one provider offered); `make_llm(cfg)` in `scraper_api.app`; `startup_notice(cfg)` in `scraper_api.__main__`.

```bash

```

Expected: PASS (output contains `nextjs_auth_hosted`)

- [ ] **Step 2: Create `tests/test_hosted_provider.py`**

A hosted API normally talks to Mistral with the operator's key. For development and for browser tests it must be able to talk to a model server on this machine instead, so that nothing is billed and no page text leaves it.

```python
import pytest

from scraper.config import Config, load
from scraper.llm import LMStudio, MistralAPI
from scraper_api.__main__ import startup_notice
from scraper_api.app import make_llm

HOSTED = {
    "SCRAPER_HOSTED": "true",
    "SCRAPER_DATABASE_URL": "postgresql://api@db/scraper",
    "SCRAPER_JWKS_URL": "https://web.test/api/auth/jwks",
    "SCRAPER_JWT_ISSUER": "https://web.test",
    "SCRAPER_JWT_AUDIENCE": "https://api.test",
    "SCRAPER_SERVICE_SECRET": "s3cret",
}


def test_hosted_mode_still_defaults_to_mistral_and_still_needs_its_key():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="SCRAPER_HOSTED needs SCRAPER_API_KEY"):
        load(HOSTED)
    cfg = load({**HOSTED, "SCRAPER_API_KEY": "key"})
    assert (cfg.provider, cfg.available_providers) == ("mistral", ("mistral",))


def test_hosted_mode_can_run_on_a_model_server_you_run_yourself_and_then_needs_no_key():
    cfg = load({**HOSTED, "SCRAPER_PROVIDER": "lmstudio", "SCRAPER_BASE_URL": "http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1"})
    assert (cfg.hosted, cfg.provider, cfg.available_providers) == (True, "lmstudio", ("lmstudio",))
    assert cfg.base_url == "http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1" and cfg.api_key == ""


def test_the_other_hosted_settings_are_still_required_with_a_local_model_server():
    env = {k: v for k, v in HOSTED.items() if k != "SCRAPER_DATABASE_URL"}
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="SCRAPER_DATABASE_URL"):
        load({**env, "SCRAPER_PROVIDER": "lmstudio"})


def test_an_unknown_provider_is_still_refused_in_hosted_mode():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="SCRAPER_PROVIDER must be one of"):
        load({**HOSTED, "SCRAPER_API_KEY": "key", "SCRAPER_PROVIDER": "gpt-nine"})


def test_make_llm_follows_the_configured_provider():
    mistral = make_llm(Config(hosted=True, provider="mistral", api_key="k", mistral_base_url="https://mistral.test/v1"))
    assert isinstance(mistral, MistralAPI) and mistral.hosted is True and mistral.base_url == "https://mistral.test/v1"
    local = make_llm(Config(hosted=True, provider="lmstudio", base_url="http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1"))
    assert isinstance(local, LMStudio) and local.hosted is False and local.base_url == "http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1"


def test_the_start_up_notice_says_where_page_text_goes():
    assert "sent to Mistral" in startup_notice(Config(hosted=True, provider="mistral", host="0.0.0.0", port=8080))
    local = startup_notice(Config(hosted=True, provider="lmstudio", base_url="http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1"))
    assert "http://127.0.0.1:1234/v1" in local and "Mistral" not in local
```

### Task 1: Put the invitation token in the address fragment

Plan 2's reviewer asked for this before any sign-up screen exists, so the screen can be written once.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/invite-link.ts`, `web/tests/invite-link.test.ts`, `web/tests/next-config.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/lib/admin-invites.ts`, `web/next.config.ts`, `web/tests/admin.test.ts`, `web/tests/email.test.ts`, `web/scripts/e2e.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `adminInviteHandlers`, `inviteMessage` and the `/sign-up` flow from plan 2.
- Produces: `buildInviteLink(baseUrl, token, email)` returning `<base>/sign-up#invite=<token>&email=<address>`; `parseInviteHash(hash)` returning `{ token, email }` or null; a `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: no-store` rule for `/sign-up`.

`web/node_modules` is not in git. This installs exactly what plan 2 pinned.

```bash
cd web && npm ci --no-audit --no-fund
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Create `web/tests/invite-link.test.ts`**

Plan 2 put the token in the query string (`?invite=`). A query string is sent to the server on every request and can reach logs and `Referer` headers. The part after `#` never leaves the browser, so the sign-up page reads the token from there.

```
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { buildInviteLink, parseInviteHash } from "../src/lib/invite-link";

const TOKEN = "Zk3pQ9vXw8LmN2pRt7YbHc5JdFg1SaUeQ0oIx6TnVwA"; // what randomBytes(32).toString("base64url") looks like

describe("invitation links", () => {
  it("put the token and the address after the # so they never reach a server log or a Referer header", () => {
    const link = buildInviteLink("https://app.example.com", TOKEN, "bob+test@example.com");
    expect(link).toBe(`https://app.example.com/sign-up#invite=${TOKEN}&email=bob%2Btest%40example.com`);
    expect(new URL(link).search).toBe(""); // nothing in the part a server sees
  });

  it("round trip through the parser", () => {
    const { hash } = new URL(buildInviteLink("https://app.example.com", TOKEN, "bob+test@example.com"));
    expect(parseInviteHash(hash)).toEqual({ invite: TOKEN, email: "bob+test@example.com" });
  });

  it("parse with or without the leading #", () => {
    expect(parseInviteHash(`invite=${TOKEN}&email=a%40x.test`)).toEqual({ invite: TOKEN, email: "a@x.test" });
  });

  it.each([
    ["nothing at all", ""],
    ["only a #", "#"],
    ["no token", "#email=a%40x.test"],
    ["no address", `#invite=${TOKEN}`],
    ["an empty token", "#invite=&email=a%40x.test"],
    ["a token with odd characters", "#invite=%3Cscript%3E%20alert%281%29&email=a%40x.test"],
    ["a very long token", `#invite=${"a".repeat(500)}&email=a%40x.test`],
    ["an address without an @", `#invite=${TOKEN}&email=not-an-address`],
    ["a very long address", `#invite=${TOKEN}&email=${"a".repeat(300)}%40x.test`],
  ])("refuse %s", (_name, hash) => {
    expect(parseInviteHash(hash)).toBeNull();
  });

  it("ignore other keys and take only what it needs", () => {
    expect(parseInviteHash(`#utm=1&invite=${TOKEN}&email=a%40x.test&next=%2F`)).toEqual({ invite: TOKEN, email: "a@x.test" });
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `web/tests/next-config.test.ts`**

```
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import config from "../next.config";

describe("next.config headers", () => {
  it("tell the browser never to send the sign-up page's address as a Referer, and not to cache it", async () => {
    const rules = await config.headers!();
    const signUp = rules.find((rule) => rule.source === "/sign-up");
    expect(signUp, "a rule for /sign-up").toBeDefined();
    const headers = Object.fromEntries(signUp!.headers.map((h) => [h.key.toLowerCase(), h.value]));
    expect(headers["referrer-policy"]).toBe("no-referrer");
    expect(headers["cache-control"]).toBe("no-store");
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Create `web/src/lib/invite-link.ts`**

```
/**
 * The address in an invitation email. The token and the person's email go after the `#`, so they are never sent to a server:
 * they stay out of request logs and out of `Referer` headers, and only the sign-up page's own script reads them.
 */

const TOKEN_SHAPE = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,128}$/; // what 32 random bytes encoded as base64url look like

export function buildInviteLink(baseUrl: string, token: string, email: string): string {
  return `${baseUrl}/sign-up#invite=${encodeURIComponent(token)}&email=${encodeURIComponent(email)}`;
}

export function parseInviteHash(hash: string): { invite: string; email: string } | null {
  const params = new URLSearchParams(hash.startsWith("#") ? hash.slice(1) : hash);
  const invite = params.get("invite") ?? "";
  const email = params.get("email") ?? "";
  if (!TOKEN_SHAPE.test(invite) || email.length > 254 || !email.includes("@")) return null;
  return { invite, email };
}
```

### Task 2: A browser test stack with a scripted model

The screens are checked in a real browser against the real web app and the real Python API. Only the model is a stand-in, and it never contacts anything. That is how a whole run, with its questions, tool calls and answers, becomes repeatable and costs nothing.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/e2e/support/fake-model.ts`, `web/e2e/support/fake-model-main.ts`, `web/e2e/support/env.ts`, `web/e2e/specs/smoke.spec.ts`, `web/playwright.config.ts`, `web/scripts/stack.sh`, `web/scripts/e2e-ui.sh`, `web/tests/fake-model.test.ts`
- Modify: `web/package.json`, `web/package-lock.json`, `web/.gitignore`, `web/scripts/e2e.sh`, `web/tests/e2e-guard.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Plan 2's `scripts/e2e.sh` (which starts the web app and the API), and Task 0's local-model provider.
- Produces: `startFakeModel(port)` and `fakeModelClient(url)` with `script(steps)`, `requests()`, `release(label)`, `reset()`; `scripts/stack.sh` with `stack_up` and `stack_down`; `npm run e2e:ui`; the libraries the screens use.

- [ ] **Step 5: Create `web/tests/fake-model.test.ts`**

The fake model server stands in for LM Studio. A test scripts what the model will say, step by step: reasoning, text, tool calls, and a `hold` that makes a step wait until the test releases it, so a test can look at the screen while a run is still working. It also records every request, so a test can check what the agent asked.

```
import { afterAll, beforeAll, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { startFakeModel, type FakeModel } from "../e2e/support/fake-model";

let model: FakeModel;
beforeAll(async () => {
  model = await startFakeModel(0);
});
afterAll(() => model.close());
beforeEach(() => model.reset());

type Chunk = { choices: { delta: { content?: string; reasoning_content?: string; tool_calls?: { index: number; id: string; function: { name: string; arguments: string } }[] }; finish_reason: string | null }[] };

async function chat(messages: unknown[] = [{ role: "user", content: "hi" }]): Promise<{ chunks: Chunk[]; raw: string }> {
  const response = await fetch(`${model.url}/v1/chat/completions`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({ model: "fake-model", messages, stream: true }),
  });
  const raw = await response.text();
  const chunks = raw
    .split("\n\n")
    .filter((frame) => frame.startsWith("data: ") && !frame.includes("[DONE]"))
    .map((frame) => JSON.parse(frame.slice(6)) as Chunk);
  return { chunks, raw };
}
const text = (chunks: Chunk[]) => chunks.map((c) => c.choices[0]?.delta.content ?? "").join("");

describe("the fake model server", () => {
  it("lists one model the way LM Studio does, in both of the places the API looks", async () => {
    const detailed = await (await fetch(`${model.url}/api/v0/models`)).json();
    expect(detailed.data[0]).toMatchObject({ id: "fake-model", type: "llm", state: "loaded", loaded_context_length: 32768 });
    const plain = await (await fetch(`${model.url}/v1/models`)).json();
    expect(plain.data[0].id).toBe("fake-model");
  });

  it("streams scripted text as small chunks and ends the stream", async () => {
    await model.script([{ text: "Ten dollars a month." }]);
    const { chunks, raw } = await chat();
    expect(text(chunks)).toBe("Ten dollars a month.");
    expect(chunks.length).toBeGreaterThan(3);
    expect(chunks.at(-1)!.choices[0].finish_reason).toBe("stop");
    expect(raw.trimEnd().endsWith("data: [DONE]")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("streams reasoning separately from the answer", async () => {
    await model.script([{ reasoning: "Let me think.", text: "Done." }]);
    const { chunks } = await chat();
    expect(chunks.map((c) => c.choices[0].delta.reasoning_content ?? "").join("")).toBe("Let me think.");
    expect(text(chunks)).toBe("Done.");
  });

  it("streams a tool call with its name and JSON arguments, and finishes with tool_calls", async () => {
    await model.script([{ toolCalls: [{ name: "add_rows", args: { rows: [{ plan: "Solo", price: "$9" }] } }] }]);
    const { chunks } = await chat();
    const call = chunks.flatMap((c) => c.choices[0].delta.tool_calls ?? [])[0];
    expect(call.function.name).toBe("add_rows");
    expect(JSON.parse(call.function.arguments)).toEqual({ rows: [{ plan: "Solo", price: "$9" }] });
    expect(call.id).toMatch(/^call_/);
    expect(chunks.at(-1)!.choices[0].finish_reason).toBe("tool_calls");
  });

  it("answers the script's steps in order, one per request, then says it has run out", async () => {
    await model.script([{ text: "first" }, { text: "second" }]);
    expect(text((await chat()).chunks)).toBe("first");
    expect(text((await chat()).chunks)).toBe("second");
    expect(text((await chat()).chunks)).toContain("script exhausted");
  });

  it("records what it was asked, so a test can check what the agent sent", async () => {
    await model.script([{ text: "ok" }]);
    await chat([{ role: "system", content: "be brief" }, { role: "user", content: "Collect the plans" }]);
    const requests = await model.requests();
    expect(requests).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(requests[0].messages.map((m) => m.role)).toEqual(["system", "user"]);
    expect(JSON.stringify(requests[0].messages)).toContain("Collect the plans");
  });

  it("holds a reply until it is released, so a test can look at the screen while a run is working", async () => {
    await model.script([{ hold: "gate", text: "released" }]);
    let finished = false;
    const pending = chat().then((result) => {
      finished = true;
      return result;
    });
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 150));
    expect(finished).toBe(false);
    await model.release("gate");
    expect(text((await pending).chunks)).toBe("released");
  });

  it("can be told to start again", async () => {
    await model.script([{ text: "a" }]);
    await chat();
    await model.reset();
    expect(await model.requests()).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Create `web/e2e/support/fake-model.ts`**

```
/**
 * A stand-in for a local model server (LM Studio's API), driven by a script. The browser tests run the real Python API against it,
 * so a whole run, with its questions, tool calls and answers, is repeatable and costs nothing. It never contacts anything.
 *
 * Each chat request takes the next step of the script. A step can stream reasoning, text and tool calls, and can hold until a test
 * releases it, which is how a test looks at the screen while a run is still working.
 */
import { createServer, type IncomingMessage, type ServerResponse } from "node:http";
import type { AddressInfo } from "node:net";

export type Step = {
  reasoning?: string;
  text?: string;
  toolCalls?: { name: string; args: unknown }[];
  /** Wait until a test calls `release(label)` before answering. */
  hold?: string;
  delayMs?: number;
};
export type RecordedRequest = { model: string; messages: { role: string; content: unknown }[]; tools: string[] };
export type FakeModel = {
  url: string;
  script(steps: Step[]): Promise<void>;
  requests(): Promise<RecordedRequest[]>;
  release(label: string): Promise<void>;
  reset(): Promise<void>;
  close(): Promise<void>;
};

const MODEL_ID = "fake-model";
const sleep = (ms: number) => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));

async function readJson(request: IncomingMessage): Promise<any> {
  const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
  for await (const chunk of request) chunks.push(chunk as Buffer);
  const text = Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8");
  return text ? JSON.parse(text) : {};
}

const send = (response: ServerResponse, status: number, body: unknown) => {
  response.writeHead(status, { "content-type": "application/json" });
  response.end(JSON.stringify(body));
};

export async function startFakeModel(port: number, host = "127.0.0.1"): Promise<FakeModel> {
  let queue: Step[] = [];
  let recorded: RecordedRequest[] = [];
  const released = new Set<string>();
  const waiters = new Map<string, (() => void)[]>();
  let callNumber = 0;

  const hold = async (label: string) => {
    if (released.has(label)) return;
    await new Promise<void>((resolve) => waiters.set(label, [...(waiters.get(label) ?? []), resolve]));
  };
  const open = (label: string) => {
    released.add(label);
    for (const resolve of waiters.get(label) ?? []) resolve();
    waiters.delete(label);
  };

  async function streamCompletion(request: IncomingMessage, response: ServerResponse) {
    const body = await readJson(request);
    recorded.push({
      model: String(body.model ?? ""),
      messages: (body.messages ?? []).map((m: { role: string; content: unknown }) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content })),
      tools: (body.tools ?? []).map((t: { function: { name: string } }) => t.function.name),
    });
    const step: Step = queue.shift() ?? { text: "(script exhausted: the test gave the model nothing more to say)" };
    if (step.hold) await hold(step.hold);
    if (step.delayMs) await sleep(step.delayMs);

    const id = `chatcmpl-${++callNumber}`;
    const frame = (delta: object, finish: string | null = null) =>
      response.write(`data: ${JSON.stringify({ id, object: "chat.completion.chunk", created: 0, model: MODEL_ID, choices: [{ index: 0, delta, finish_reason: finish }] })}\n\n`);
    response.writeHead(200, { "content-type": "text/event-stream", "cache-control": "no-store" });
    frame({ role: "assistant", content: "" });
    for (const piece of step.reasoning?.match(/\S+\s*|\s+/g) ?? []) frame({ reasoning_content: piece });
    for (const piece of step.text?.match(/\S+\s*|\s+/g) ?? []) frame({ content: piece });
    (step.toolCalls ?? []).forEach((call, index) =>
      frame({ tool_calls: [{ index, id: `call_${id}_${index}`, type: "function", function: { name: call.name, arguments: JSON.stringify(call.args) } }] }),
    );
    frame({}, step.toolCalls?.length ? "tool_calls" : "stop");
    response.write("data: [DONE]\n\n");
    response.end();
  }

  const server = createServer((request, response) => {
    const url = new URL(request.url ?? "/", "http://fake");
    const route = `${request.method} ${url.pathname}`;
    const run = async () => {
      if (route === "GET /api/v0/models") return send(response, 200, { data: [{ id: MODEL_ID, type: "llm", state: "loaded", loaded_context_length: 32768 }] });
      if (route === "GET /v1/models") return send(response, 200, { data: [{ id: MODEL_ID }] });
      if (route === "POST /v1/chat/completions") return streamCompletion(request, response);
      if (route === "POST /__script") {
        queue = ((await readJson(request)).steps ?? []) as Step[];
        return send(response, 200, { queued: queue.length });
      }
      if (route === "GET /__requests") return send(response, 200, { requests: recorded });
      if (route === "POST /__reset") {
        queue = [];
        recorded = [];
        for (const label of [...waiters.keys()]) open(label);
        released.clear();
        return send(response, 200, {});
      }
      const gate = /^POST \/__release\/(.+)$/.exec(route);
      if (gate) {
        open(decodeURIComponent(gate[1]));
        return send(response, 200, {});
      }
      send(response, 404, { error: `no route ${route}` });
    };
    run().catch((error) => {
      if (!response.headersSent) send(response, 500, { error: String(error) });
      else response.end();
    });
  });

  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => server.listen(port, host, resolve));
  const url = `http://${host}:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}`;
  const call = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => fetch(`${url}${path}`, init);
  return {
    url,
    script: async (steps) => void (await call("/__script", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ steps }) })),
    requests: async () => ((await (await call("/__requests")).json()) as { requests: RecordedRequest[] }).requests,
    release: async (label) => void (await call(`/__release/${encodeURIComponent(label)}`, { method: "POST" })),
    reset: async () => void (await call("/__reset", { method: "POST" })),
    close: () => new Promise<void>((resolve) => (server.closeAllConnections(), server.close(() => resolve()))),
  };
}

/** The same controls for a fake model started elsewhere (by scripts/e2e-ui.sh), reached over HTTP. */
export function fakeModelClient(url: string): Omit<FakeModel, "close"> {
  const post = (path: string, body?: unknown) => fetch(`${url}${path}`, { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body) });
  return {
    url,
    script: async (steps) => void (await post("/__script", { steps })),
    requests: async () => ((await (await fetch(`${url}/__requests`)).json()) as { requests: RecordedRequest[] }).requests,
    release: async (label) => void (await post(`/__release/${encodeURIComponent(label)}`)),
    reset: async () => void (await post("/__reset")),
  };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Create `web/tests/e2e-guard.test.ts`**

Both end-to-end scripts drop database schemas first, so each must refuse any database whose name does not contain "test", and must refuse to start without `E2E_DATABASE_URL`. This test runs the scripts and checks that they do.

```
import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

// The end-to-end script drops the `auth` and `app` schemas of the database it is given, so its guard must look at the database
// NAME, not at the whole address. Every address here points at a closed port, so nothing could be touched even if the guard failed.
const SCRIPTS = ["scripts/e2e.sh", "scripts/e2e-ui.sh"];
const run = (script: string, url: string) => spawnSync("bash", [script], { env: { ...process.env, E2E_DATABASE_URL: url }, encoding: "utf8", timeout: 60_000 });

describe.each(SCRIPTS)("%s database guard", (script) => {
  it.each([
    ["a user named like a test", "postgresql://tester@127.0.0.1:1/production"],
    ["a host named like a test", "postgresql://owner@test-db.internal:1/production"],
    ["a password containing test", "postgresql://owner:test1234@127.0.0.1:1/production"],
    ["a query string containing test", "postgresql://owner@127.0.0.1:1/production?application_name=test"],
    ["no database name at all", "postgresql://owner@test-host:1"],
    ["a socket-style address with a real name", "postgresql://owner@/production?host=/tmp/test"],
  ])("refuses %s", (_name, url) => {
    const result = run(script, url);
    expect(result.status).toBe(2);
    expect(result.stderr).toMatch(/database name/);
  });

  it("lets a database whose name contains test get past the guard", () => {
    const result = run(script, "postgresql://owner@127.0.0.1:1/scraper_e2e_test"); // fails later, on the closed port, which is the point
    expect(result.stderr).not.toMatch(/database name/);
  });

  it("asks for the address when none is given", () => {
    const result = spawnSync("bash", [script], { env: { ...process.env, E2E_DATABASE_URL: "" }, encoding: "utf8", timeout: 60_000 });
    expect(result.status).not.toBe(0);
    expect(result.stderr).toMatch(/E2E_DATABASE_URL/);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 12: Create `web/scripts/stack.sh`**

The part of `e2e.sh` that starts the stack, shared so that both checks use it. It starts the web app in development mode (a production server insists on https and a real email service), the fake model, and the Python API with `SCRAPER_PROVIDER=lmstudio` pointed at the fake model, from a directory with no `.env`, so the key in the repository's `.env` cannot be picked up. It starts the web app from an empty `.next`, because a build cache left by an earlier session can make the page reload over and over under a test.

```bash
# Starts the whole stack on one scratch database: the web app, the Python API and, for the browser tests, a scripted fake model.
# Not run directly: scripts/e2e.sh and scripts/e2e-ui.sh source it.
#
#   STACK_PROVIDER=mistral   the API is given a placeholder key and an address that refuses connections (nothing can reach Mistral)
#   STACK_PROVIDER=lmstudio  the API talks to the fake model server this script starts (nothing can reach a real model either)
#
# After `stack_up`: WEB_URL, API_URL, MODEL_URL (lmstudio only) and LOGS are set. Call `stack_down` on exit.

stack_up() {
  if [ -z "${E2E_DATABASE_URL:-}" ]; then
    echo "set E2E_DATABASE_URL to a scratch database (its auth and app schemas are dropped)" >&2
    exit 2
  fi
  # Look at the database NAME only (between the first / after the host and any ?): a user, host or password that happens to say "test"
  # must not unlock a script that drops schemas.
  local rest=${E2E_DATABASE_URL#*://} name
  case "$rest" in */*) name=${rest#*/}; name=${name%%[?#]*} ;; *) name="" ;; esac
  case "$name" in *test*) ;; *) echo "refusing: the database name (\"$name\") must contain 'test', because this script drops its schemas" >&2; exit 2;; esac

  WEB_PORT=${WEB_PORT:-3100}; API_PORT=${API_PORT:-8180}; MODEL_PORT=${MODEL_PORT:-8190}
  WEB_URL="http://localhost:$WEB_PORT"; API_URL="http://localhost:$API_PORT"; MODEL_URL="http://127.0.0.1:$MODEL_PORT"
  for port in "$WEB_PORT" "$API_PORT" "$MODEL_PORT"; do  # a leftover server on these ports would be tested by mistake
    if lsof -ti "tcp:$port" -sTCP:LISTEN > /dev/null 2>&1; then echo "port $port is already in use; stop that server or set WEB_PORT / API_PORT / MODEL_PORT" >&2; exit 2; fi
  done
  ROOT=$(cd .. && pwd)
  LOGS=$(mktemp -d)
  SECRET=$(node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(24).toString('base64url'))")

  psql "$E2E_DATABASE_URL" -q -c "DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS auth CASCADE" -c "DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS app CASCADE" -c "CREATE SCHEMA auth"
  export DATABASE_URL="$E2E_DATABASE_URL" BETTER_AUTH_SECRET="$(node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('base64url'))")" \
    BETTER_AUTH_URL="$WEB_URL" API_URL="$API_URL" API_AUDIENCE="$API_URL" SERVICE_SECRET="$SECRET" \
    EMAIL_TRANSPORT=log ADMIN_EMAIL=owner@example.com
  npm run --silent migrate

  # The development server, not `next start`: a production server insists on https and a real email service, which is right
  # for a deployment and wrong for a check over plain http on this machine.
  # It starts from an empty build cache: a cache left by an earlier session can make the page reload over and over under a test.
  rm -rf .next
  npx next dev -p "$WEB_PORT" > "$LOGS/web.log" 2>&1 &

  # Nothing here may reach a real model provider, and the API runs from a directory with no .env, so the key in the repository's
  # .env cannot be picked up.
  local provider_env
  if [ "${STACK_PROVIDER:-mistral}" = lmstudio ]; then
    FAKE_MODEL_PORT="$MODEL_PORT" npx tsx e2e/support/fake-model-main.ts > "$LOGS/model.log" 2>&1 &
    provider_env="SCRAPER_PROVIDER=lmstudio SCRAPER_BASE_URL=$MODEL_URL/v1"
  else
    provider_env="SCRAPER_API_KEY=not-a-real-key SCRAPER_MISTRAL_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:1/v1"
  fi
  (cd "$LOGS" && exec env $provider_env SCRAPER_HOSTED=true SCRAPER_DATABASE_URL="$E2E_DATABASE_URL" SCRAPER_JWKS_URL="$WEB_URL/api/auth/jwks" \
    SCRAPER_JWT_ISSUER="$WEB_URL" SCRAPER_JWT_AUDIENCE="$API_URL" SCRAPER_SERVICE_SECRET="$SECRET" SCRAPER_CORS_ORIGINS="$WEB_URL" \
    SCRAPER_HOST=127.0.0.1 SCRAPER_PORT="$API_PORT" uv run --project "$ROOT" python -m scraper_api > "$LOGS/api.log" 2>&1) &

  local urls=("$WEB_URL/" "$API_URL/health")
  [ "${STACK_PROVIDER:-mistral}" = lmstudio ] && urls+=("$MODEL_URL/v1/models")
  for url in "${urls[@]}"; do
    for _ in $(seq 1 150); do curl -fs -o /dev/null "$url" 2>/dev/null && break; sleep 0.2; done
    curl -fs -o /dev/null "$url" 2>/dev/null || { echo "did not start: $url"; tail -20 "$LOGS"/*.log; exit 1; }
  done
}

stack_down() {
  local status=$?  # the script's own exit status: cleanup must hand it on unchanged
  trap - EXIT
  # `uv run` and `npx` start the real server as a child; stop the children too
  for pid in $(jobs -p); do pkill -TERM -P "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true; kill "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true; done
  sleep 0.5
  if [ -n "${LOGS:-}" ]; then rm -rf "$LOGS"; fi
  exit "$status"
}
```

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run the browser tests (Playwright) against the whole stack, with a scripted fake model standing in for the model server.
#
#   E2E_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user@host/scraper_e2e_test npm run e2e:ui [-- playwright arguments]
#
# The database must be a scratch one: the script drops its `auth` and `app` schemas first. Nothing here contacts a real model.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
source scripts/stack.sh
trap stack_down EXIT
STACK_PROVIDER=lmstudio stack_up

status=0
E2E_RESULTS_DIR="$LOGS/test-results" WEB_URL="$WEB_URL" API_URL="$API_URL" FAKE_MODEL_URL="$MODEL_URL" WEB_LOG="$LOGS/web.log" npx playwright test "$@" || status=$?
if [ "$status" != 0 ] || [ -n "${E2E_SHOW_LOGS:-}" ]; then echo "--- web log"; tail -${E2E_LOG_LINES:-15} "$LOGS/web.log"; echo "--- api log"; tail -15 "$LOGS/api.log"; fi
exit $status
```

- [ ] **Step 15: Create `web/playwright.config.ts`**

Test results are written outside the project: the web app runs in development mode, and files written inside it make it reload the page under the test.

```
import { defineConfig, devices } from "@playwright/test";

// Run through `npm run e2e:ui`, which starts the web app, the Python API and a fake model first and sets these addresses.
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: "./e2e/specs",
  // Outside the project: the web app runs in development mode, and files written inside it make it reload the page under the test.
  outputDir: process.env.E2E_RESULTS_DIR ?? "./test-results",
  workers: 1, // the specs share one database and one fake model
  fullyParallel: false,
  retries: 0,
  timeout: 60_000,
  expect: { timeout: 10_000 },
  reporter: [["list"]],
  use: { baseURL: process.env.WEB_URL, trace: "retain-on-failure", screenshot: "only-on-failure" },
  projects: [{ name: "chromium", use: { ...devices["Desktop Chrome"] } }],
});
```

```
/** The addresses scripts/e2e-ui.sh sets for the browser tests. */
const need = (name: string): string => {
  const value = process.env[name];
  if (!value) throw new Error(`${name} is not set. Run the browser tests with \`npm run e2e:ui\`, which starts the stack and sets it.`);
  return value;
};

export const env = {
  get web() {
    return need("WEB_URL");
  },
  get api() {
    return need("API_URL");
  },
  get model() {
    return need("FAKE_MODEL_URL");
  },
  get webLog() {
    return need("WEB_LOG");
  },
  get database() {
    return need("DATABASE_URL");
  },
};
```

### Task 3: Sign in, sign up, and reset a password

Every account screen, in the approved design. The workspace comes in Task 7; until then the home page says who is signed in and offers to sign out.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/auth-errors.ts`, `web/src/lib/password.ts`, `web/src/lib/auth-client.ts`, `web/src/lib/session.ts`, `web/src/proxy.ts`, `web/src/app/globals.css`
- Create: `web/src/components/SignOutButton.tsx`, `web/src/components/auth/` (six components), the pages `sign-in`, `sign-up`, `forgot-password`, `reset-password` and `verify-email` under `web/src/app/`
- Create: `web/tests/auth-errors.test.ts`, `web/tests/password.test.ts`, `web/tests/proxy.test.ts`, `web/e2e/support/journeys.ts`, `web/e2e/specs/auth.spec.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/app/layout.tsx`, `web/src/app/page.tsx`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Better Auth's routes and the invitation link from Tasks 1 and plan 2; the browser test stack from Task 2.
- Produces: `describeAuthError(error, context)`; `passwordProblem(password)` (12 to 128 characters); `proxy` (a cookie check that sends signed-out visitors from `/` and `/admin` to `/sign-in`); `authClient`; `getSession()`, `requireSession()`, `requireAdmin()`; every account page; the design tokens in `globals.css`; browser helpers `createMember`, `signInBrowser`, `ownerCookie`, `inviteLink`, `emailLink`, `alertSaying`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Create `web/tests/auth-errors.test.ts`**

Better Auth answers with codes and English messages meant for developers. The screens never show them: `describeAuthError` turns each code into a sentence that says what went wrong and what to do, and any code it does not know into one generic sentence.

```
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { describeAuthError } from "../src/lib/auth-errors";

describe("describeAuthError", () => {
  it("explains a wrong email or password without saying which one is wrong", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "INVALID_EMAIL_OR_PASSWORD", status: 401 }, "sign-in")).toMatch(/email address or password is not right/i);
  });

  it("tells someone whose address is unconfirmed what to do", () => {
    const text = describeAuthError({ code: "EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED", status: 403 }, "sign-in");
    expect(text).toMatch(/confirm your email address/i);
  });

  it("says a suspended account is suspended", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "BANNED_USER", status: 403 }, "sign-in")).toMatch(/suspended/i);
  });

  it("explains an invitation that does not work, and what to do next", () => {
    const text = describeAuthError({ code: "INVITE_REQUIRED", status: 403 }, "sign-up");
    expect(text).toMatch(/invitation/i);
    expect(text).toMatch(/new one/i);
  });

  it("points an existing account to sign in", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "USER_ALREADY_EXISTS", status: 422 }, "sign-up")).toMatch(/sign in/i);
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "USER_ALREADY_EXISTS_USE_ANOTHER_EMAIL", status: 422 }, "sign-up")).toMatch(/sign in/i);
  });

  it("says when to try again after too many attempts", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ status: 429 }, "sign-in")).toMatch(/too many attempts/i);
  });

  it("explains a reset link that no longer works", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "INVALID_TOKEN", status: 400 }, "reset")).toMatch(/link/i);
  });

  it("explains a refused deletion", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "ACCOUNT_DATA_NOT_DELETED", status: 409, message: "A run is still stopping. Try again in a few seconds." }, "delete")).toMatch(/try again/i);
    expect(describeAuthError({ code: "PASSWORD_REQUIRED", status: 400 }, "delete")).toMatch(/password/i);
  });

  it("never shows a raw code or an unknown server message", () => {
    const text = describeAuthError({ code: "SOMETHING_NEW", status: 500, message: "ECONNREFUSED db.internal:5432" }, "sign-in");
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/SOMETHING_NEW|ECONNREFUSED|5432/);
    expect(text).toMatch(/something went wrong/i);
  });

  it("copes with no error details at all", () => {
    expect(describeAuthError(null, "sign-in")).toMatch(/something went wrong/i);
    expect(describeAuthError(undefined, "sign-up")).toMatch(/something went wrong/i);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Create `web/tests/password.test.ts`**

```
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { MAX_PASSWORD, MIN_PASSWORD, passwordProblem } from "../src/lib/password";

describe("passwordProblem", () => {
  it("accepts a passphrase of the right length", () => {
    expect(passwordProblem("correct horse battery")).toBeNull();
    expect(passwordProblem("a".repeat(MIN_PASSWORD))).toBeNull();
    expect(passwordProblem("a".repeat(MAX_PASSWORD))).toBeNull();
  });

  it("says how many characters are needed when it is too short", () => {
    expect(passwordProblem("short")).toBe(`Use at least ${MIN_PASSWORD} characters. A few ordinary words work well.`);
    expect(passwordProblem("a".repeat(MIN_PASSWORD - 1))).not.toBeNull();
  });

  it("refuses an absurdly long one", () => {
    expect(passwordProblem("a".repeat(MAX_PASSWORD + 1))).toMatch(/at most/);
  });

  it("counts characters people see, not UTF-16 units", () => {
    expect(passwordProblem("🔑".repeat(MIN_PASSWORD))).toBeNull(); // each emoji is two units but one character
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `web/tests/proxy.test.ts`**

The proxy is a fast first gate: it only checks that a session cookie exists, and sends everyone else to sign in. Pages still verify the session on the server; the proxy makes the common case quick.

```
import { NextRequest } from "next/server";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { config, hasSessionCookie, proxy } from "../src/proxy";

const request = (path: string, cookie?: string) => new NextRequest(`http://localhost:3100${path}`, { headers: cookie ? { cookie } : {} });

describe("hasSessionCookie", () => {
  it.each([
    ["better-auth.session_token=abc", true],
    ["__Secure-better-auth.session_token=abc", true], // the name in production
    ["theme=dark; better-auth.session_token=abc", true],
    ["better-auth.session_data=abc", false], // only a cache of the session
    ["better-auth.session_token=", false],
    ["other=1", false],
    ["", false],
  ])("%j -> %s", (cookie, expected) => {
    const names = new Request("http://x", { headers: cookie ? { cookie } : {} });
    expect(hasSessionCookie(names.headers.get("cookie") ?? "")).toBe(expected);
  });
});

describe("proxy", () => {
  it("sends a visitor with no session cookie from the workspace and the admin page to sign in", () => {
    for (const path of ["/", "/admin"]) {
      const response = proxy(request(path));
      expect(response.status, path).toBe(307);
      expect(new URL(response.headers.get("location")!).pathname).toBe("/sign-in");
    }
  });

  it("lets a visitor with a session cookie through (the page itself checks the session for real)", () => {
    const response = proxy(request("/", "better-auth.session_token=abc"));
    expect(response.headers.get("location")).toBeNull();
    expect(response.status).toBe(200);
  });

  it("only guards the pages that need a session", () => {
    expect(config.matcher).toEqual(["/", "/admin/:path*"]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Create `web/src/lib/auth-errors.ts`**

```
/** What to tell a person when Better Auth or our hooks refuse something. Never shows a raw code or a server message. */

export type AuthContext = "sign-in" | "sign-up" | "forgot" | "reset" | "delete";
type AuthFailure = { code?: string; status?: number; message?: string } | null | undefined;

const GENERIC = "Something went wrong. Try again in a moment.";

export function describeAuthError(error: AuthFailure, context: AuthContext): string {
  if (!error) return GENERIC;
  if (error.status === 429) return "Too many attempts. Wait a minute and try again.";
  switch (error.code) {
    case "INVALID_EMAIL_OR_PASSWORD":
      return "The email address or password is not right. Check both and try again.";
    case "EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED":
      return "Confirm your email address first. We sent you a link when you signed up.";
    case "BANNED_USER":
      return "This account has been suspended. Ask the person who invited you for help.";
    case "INVITE_REQUIRED":
      return "This invitation is not valid. It may have expired or already been used. Ask for a new one.";
    case "USER_ALREADY_EXISTS":
    case "USER_ALREADY_EXISTS_USE_ANOTHER_EMAIL":
      return "That address already has an account. Sign in instead.";
    case "PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT":
      return "Use at least 12 characters. A few ordinary words work well.";
    case "PASSWORD_TOO_LONG":
      return "That password is too long. Use at most 128 characters.";
    case "INVALID_TOKEN":
    case "TOKEN_EXPIRED":
      return context === "reset" ? "This link has expired or was already used. Ask for a new one." : "This link is not valid any more.";
    case "PASSWORD_REQUIRED":
      return "Enter your password to delete your account.";
    case "INVALID_PASSWORD":
      return context === "delete" ? "That password is not right." : "The email address or password is not right. Check both and try again.";
    case "ACCOUNT_DATA_NOT_DELETED":
      return error.status === 409
        ? "A run is still stopping, so nothing was deleted. Try again in a few seconds."
        : "Your saved data could not be deleted, so nothing was deleted. Try again shortly.";
    default:
      return GENERIC;
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Create `web/src/lib/password.ts`**

```
export const MIN_PASSWORD = 12;
export const MAX_PASSWORD = 128;

/** What is wrong with a new password, in words for the person, or null when it is fine. Counts characters people see. */
export function passwordProblem(password: string): string | null {
  const length = [...password].length;
  if (length < MIN_PASSWORD) return `Use at least ${MIN_PASSWORD} characters. A few ordinary words work well.`;
  if (length > MAX_PASSWORD) return `Use at most ${MAX_PASSWORD} characters.`;
  return null;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Create `web/src/proxy.ts`**

```
import { NextResponse, type NextRequest } from "next/server";

/**
 * A quick, optimistic check that costs no database call: someone with no session cookie at all has no business on the workspace
 * or the administrator page, so send them to sign in. It does not prove the session is real; each of those pages checks that for itself.
 * The cookie is `better-auth.session_token`, with a `__Secure-` prefix in production.
 */
export function hasSessionCookie(cookieHeader: string): boolean {
  return cookieHeader.split(";").some((part) => {
    const [name, ...value] = part.trim().split("=");
    return name.endsWith("better-auth.session_token") && value.join("=").length > 0;
  });
}

export function proxy(request: NextRequest) {
  if (hasSessionCookie(request.headers.get("cookie") ?? "")) return NextResponse.next();
  return NextResponse.redirect(new URL("/sign-in", request.url));
}

export const config = { matcher: ["/", "/admin/:path*"] };
```

```
/* Design tokens from the approved mock. Light is the default; dark follows the system. The yellow highlighter means one thing only:

:root {
  --paper: #F3F5F3;
  --surface: #FFFFFF;
  --sunken: #E8ECE9;
  --ink: #14201B;
  --muted: #55645D;
  --rule: #D1D8D4;
  --accent: #1B5A4A;
  --accent-ink: #FFFFFF;
  --accent-soft: #DAEAE3;
  --mark: #F2D94E;
  --mark-soft: #FAEFB4;
  --bad: #A63227;
  --bad-soft: #F5DDD9;
  --warn: #8A5F00;
  --warn-soft: #F6E9C8;
  --ok: #1D7347;
  --ok-soft: #D8EEDF;
  --shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(20, 32, 27, 0.08), 0 10px 28px rgba(20, 32, 27, 0.07);
  --step--1: 0.8125rem;
  --step-0: 0.9375rem;
  --step-1: 1.0625rem;
  --step-2: 1.3125rem;
  --step-3: 1.875rem;
  color-scheme: light;
}
@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
  :root {
    --paper: #171F1D; --surface: #202A27; --sunken: #2A3633; --ink: #E7E3D6; --muted: #A5ADA4; --rule: #3A4945;
    --accent: #8ED0B4; --accent-ink: #0E1A16; --accent-soft: #28403A; --mark: #E6D04A; --mark-soft: #4A4524;
    --bad: #E8776F; --bad-soft: #4A2A27; --warn: #E3B35B; --warn-soft: #473A1D; --ok: #86C79A; --ok-soft: #26402F;
    --shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4), 0 12px 30px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.35);
    color-scheme: dark;
  }
}

* { box-sizing: border-box; }
html, body { height: 100%; }
body {
  margin: 0;
  background: var(--paper);
  color: var(--ink);
  font-family: var(--font-ui), system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif;
  font-size: var(--step-0);
  line-height: 1.5;
  -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;
}
button, input, select, textarea { font: inherit; color: inherit; }
button { cursor: pointer; }
:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 2px; border-radius: 4px; }
[hidden] { display: none !important; }
h1, h2, h3 { margin: 0; text-wrap: balance; }
p { margin: 0; }
a { color: var(--accent); text-underline-offset: 3px; }
.sr { position: absolute; width: 1px; height: 1px; overflow: hidden; clip-path: inset(50%); white-space: nowrap; }

.page { min-height: 100%; display: flex; flex-direction: column; }
.topbar { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 1rem; padding: 0.7rem 1.25rem; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--paper); }
.wordmark { font-family: var(--font-read), Georgia, serif; font-weight: 650; font-size: 1.3rem; letter-spacing: -0.01em; margin-right: auto; color: var(--ink); text-decoration: none; }

.btn {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 0.4rem;
  padding: 0.5rem 0.95rem; border-radius: 8px; border: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--surface); font-weight: 560;
  transition: background-color 120ms ease, border-color 120ms ease;
}
.btn:hover { border-color: var(--muted); }
.btn-primary { background: var(--accent); border-color: var(--accent); color: var(--accent-ink); }
.btn-primary:hover { filter: brightness(1.08); border-color: var(--accent); }
.btn-quiet { background: transparent; border-color: transparent; color: var(--muted); }
.btn-quiet:hover { color: var(--ink); background: var(--sunken); border-color: transparent; }
.btn-danger { color: var(--bad); }
.btn-small { padding: 0.25rem 0.6rem; font-size: var(--step--1); border-radius: 6px; }
.btn[disabled] { opacity: 0.55; cursor: not-allowed; }
.link { color: var(--accent); background: none; border: 0; padding: 0; text-decoration: underline; text-underline-offset: 3px; }

input[type="text"], input[type="email"], input[type="password"], select {
  border: 1px solid var(--rule); background: var(--paper); border-radius: 8px; padding: 0.42rem 0.6rem; color: var(--ink);
}

/* Sign in, sign up and the other account screens */
.auth { flex: 1; display: grid; place-items: start center; padding: 4rem 1.25rem; }
.auth-box { width: min(100%, 26rem); display: grid; gap: 1.2rem; }
.auth-box h1 { font-family: var(--font-read), Georgia, serif; font-size: var(--step-3); line-height: 1.15; font-weight: 600; }
.auth-box .lede { color: var(--muted); }
.form { display: grid; gap: 0.9rem; }
.form label { display: grid; gap: 0.3rem; font-weight: 560; font-size: var(--step--1); }
.form .hint { font-weight: 400; color: var(--muted); }
.form input { width: 100%; font-size: var(--step-0); padding: 0.55rem 0.7rem; }
.form input[readonly] { background: var(--sunken); color: var(--muted); }
.form .actions { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 1rem; flex-wrap: wrap; }
.note-box { padding: 0.7rem 0.9rem; border-radius: 8px; background: var(--ok-soft); }
.error-box { padding: 0.7rem 0.9rem; border-radius: 8px; background: var(--bad-soft); }
.stack { display: grid; gap: 0.6rem; }

/* Pieces both the workspace and the administrator page use */
.muted { color: var(--muted); }
.small { font-size: var(--step--1); }
.toast {
  position: fixed; left: 50%; bottom: 1.25rem; transform: translateX(-50%); z-index: 50; background: var(--ink); color: var(--paper);
  padding: 0.6rem 1rem; border-radius: 8px; box-shadow: var(--shadow); max-width: min(92vw, 28rem);
}
dialog { border: 1px solid var(--rule); border-radius: 12px; background: var(--surface); color: var(--ink); padding: 1.25rem; width: min(92vw, 26rem); box-shadow: var(--shadow); }
dialog::backdrop { background: rgba(10, 16, 14, 0.5); }
dialog h2 { font-family: var(--font-read), Georgia, serif; font-size: var(--step-2); font-weight: 600; margin-bottom: 0.5rem; }
dialog .actions { display: flex; justify-content: flex-end; gap: 0.5rem; margin-top: 1.1rem; flex-wrap: wrap; }

.placeholder { padding: 2rem 1.25rem; display: grid; gap: 1rem; max-width: 40rem; }

@media (max-width: 900px) {
  .topbar { padding-inline: 1rem; }
  .auth { padding-top: 2rem; }
}
```

```
import { adminClient, jwtClient } from "better-auth/client/plugins";
import { createAuthClient } from "better-auth/react";

/** The browser's way to talk to Better Auth on this same site (`/api/auth`). Only imported by client components. */
export const authClient = createAuthClient({ plugins: [jwtClient(), adminClient()] });
```

- [ ] **Step 12: Create `web/src/lib/session.ts`**

`getSession` is cached for the request with React's `cache`, so a page and its children can each ask without a second lookup.

```
import { cache } from "react";
import { headers } from "next/headers";
import { redirect } from "next/navigation";
import { getServices } from "./services";

/** The signed-in person for this request, or null. Cached for the request, so every part of a page may ask. */
export const getSession = cache(async () => getServices().auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() }));

export async function requireSession() {
  const session = await getSession();
  if (!session) redirect("/sign-in");
  return session;
}

export async function requireAdmin() {
  const session = await requireSession();
  const user = session.user as { role?: string | null; banned?: boolean | null };
  if (user.role !== "admin" || user.banned) redirect("/");
  return session;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 15: Create `web/src/components/auth/ForgotPasswordForm.tsx`**

```
"use client";

import Link from "next/link";
import { useState, type FormEvent } from "react";
import { authClient } from "../../lib/auth-client";
import { describeAuthError } from "../../lib/auth-errors";

export function ForgotPasswordForm() {
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
  const [pending, setPending] = useState(false);
  const [sent, setSent] = useState(false);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  async function submit(event: FormEvent) {
    event.preventDefault();
    setPending(true);
    setError(null);
    const { error: failure } = await authClient.requestPasswordReset({ email, redirectTo: "/reset-password" });
    setPending(false);
    // The same answer whether or not the address has an account, so this page cannot be used to find out who does.
    if (failure?.status === 429) return setError(describeAuthError(failure, "forgot"));
    setSent(true);
  }

  if (sent) {
    return (
      <>
        <h1>Check your inbox</h1>
        <p className="lede">If {email} has an account, we sent a link to choose a new password. It works once and expires in an hour.</p>
        <div>
          <Link className="btn" href="/sign-in">
            Back to sign in
          </Link>
        </div>
      </>
    );
  }

  return (
    <>
      <div className="stack">
        <h1>Reset your password</h1>
        <p className="lede">Enter your email address and we will send you a link to choose a new password.</p>
      </div>
      <form className="form" onSubmit={submit}>
        <label htmlFor="email">
          Email address
          <input id="email" name="email" type="email" autoComplete="username" required value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
        </label>
        {error ? (
          <div className="error-box" role="alert">
            {error}
          </div>
        ) : null}
        <div className="actions">
          <button className="btn btn-primary" type="submit" disabled={pending}>
            {pending ? "Sending" : "Send the link"}
          </button>
          <Link href="/sign-in">Back to sign in</Link>
        </div>
      </form>
    </>
  );
}
```

```
"use client";

import { useState, type FormEvent } from "react";
import { authClient } from "../../lib/auth-client";

export function ResendConfirmation() {
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
  const [sent, setSent] = useState(false);

  async function submit(event: FormEvent) {
    event.preventDefault();
    await authClient.sendVerificationEmail({ email, callbackURL: "/verify-email" });
    setSent(true); // the same answer for any address
  }

  if (sent) return <div className="note-box">If {email} has an account waiting to be confirmed, we sent a new link.</div>;
  return (
    <form className="form" onSubmit={submit}>
      <label htmlFor="email">
        Email address
        <input id="email" name="email" type="email" autoComplete="username" required value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
      </label>
      <div className="actions">
        <button className="btn btn-primary" type="submit">
          Send a new link
        </button>
      </div>
    </form>
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 17: Create `web/src/components/auth/ResetPasswordForm.tsx`**

```
"use client";

import Link from "next/link";
import { useRouter, useSearchParams } from "next/navigation";
import { useState, type FormEvent } from "react";
import { authClient } from "../../lib/auth-client";
import { describeAuthError } from "../../lib/auth-errors";
import { passwordProblem } from "../../lib/password";

export function ResetPasswordForm() {
  const router = useRouter();
  const params = useSearchParams();
  const token = params.get("token");
  const [password, setPassword] = useState("");
  const [pending, setPending] = useState(false);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  if (!token || params.get("error")) {
    return (
      <>
        <h1>This link has expired</h1>
        <p className="lede">Reset links work once and expire after an hour. Ask for a new one.</p>
        <div>
          <Link className="btn" href="/forgot-password">
            Send a new link
          </Link>
        </div>
      </>
    );
  }

  async function submit(event: FormEvent) {
    event.preventDefault();
    const problem = passwordProblem(password);
    if (problem) return setError(problem);
    setPending(true);
    setError(null);
    const { error: failure } = await authClient.resetPassword({ newPassword: password, token: token as string });
    if (failure) {
      setError(describeAuthError(failure, "reset"));
      setPending(false);
      return;
    }
    router.replace("/sign-in?reset=1");
  }

  return (
    <>
      <div className="stack">
        <h1>Choose a new password</h1>
        <p className="lede">You will be signed out everywhere else.</p>
      </div>
      <form className="form" onSubmit={submit}>
        <label htmlFor="password">
          New password
          <input id="password" name="password" type="password" autoComplete="new-password" required value={password} onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
          <span className="hint">At least 12 characters. A few ordinary words work well.</span>
        </label>
        {error ? (
          <div className="error-box" role="alert">
            {error}
          </div>
        ) : null}
        <div className="actions">
          <button className="btn btn-primary" type="submit" disabled={pending}>
            {pending ? "Saving" : "Save new password"}
          </button>
        </div>
      </form>
    </>
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 18: Create `web/src/components/auth/SignInForm.tsx`**

```
"use client";

import Link from "next/link";
import { useRouter } from "next/navigation";
import { useState, type FormEvent } from "react";
import { authClient } from "../../lib/auth-client";
import { describeAuthError } from "../../lib/auth-errors";

const NOTICES = {
  deleted: "Your account was deleted.",
  reset: "Your password was changed. Sign in with the new one.",
} as const;

export function SignInForm({ notice }: { notice: keyof typeof NOTICES | null }) {
  const router = useRouter();
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
  const [password, setPassword] = useState("");
  const [pending, setPending] = useState(false);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [unconfirmed, setUnconfirmed] = useState(false);
  const [resent, setResent] = useState(false);

  async function submit(event: FormEvent) {
    event.preventDefault();
    setPending(true);
    setError(null);
    setUnconfirmed(false);
    setResent(false);
    const { error: failure } = await authClient.signIn.email({ email, password });
    if (failure) {
      setError(describeAuthError(failure, "sign-in"));
      setUnconfirmed(failure.code === "EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED");
      setPending(false);
      return;
    }
    router.replace("/");
    router.refresh();
  }

  async function sendAgain() {
    await authClient.sendVerificationEmail({ email, callbackURL: "/verify-email" });
    setResent(true);
  }

  return (
    <>
      <div className="stack">
        <h1>Sign in</h1>
        <p className="lede">Tell Page Chat what to collect from a website, and it reads the pages and hands you a table.</p>
      </div>
      {notice ? <div className="note-box">{NOTICES[notice]}</div> : null}
      <form className="form" onSubmit={submit}>
        <label htmlFor="email">
          Email address
          <input id="email" name="email" type="email" autoComplete="username" required value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
        </label>
        <label htmlFor="password">
          Password
          <input id="password" name="password" type="password" autoComplete="current-password" required value={password} onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
        </label>
        {error ? (
          <div className="error-box" role="alert">
            {error}
            {unconfirmed ? (
              <>
                {" "}
                <button className="link" type="button" onClick={sendAgain}>
                  Send the link again
                </button>
              </>
            ) : null}
          </div>
        ) : null}
        {resent ? <div className="note-box">We sent a new confirmation link to {email}.</div> : null}
        <div className="actions">
          <button className="btn btn-primary" type="submit" disabled={pending}>
            {pending ? "Signing in" : "Sign in"}
          </button>
          <Link href="/forgot-password">Forgot your password?</Link>
        </div>
      </form>
      <p className="lede">Accounts are by invitation. Ask the person who runs this site to invite you.</p>
    </>
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 19: Create `web/src/components/auth/SignUpForm.tsx`**

```
"use client";

import Link from "next/link";
import { useEffect, useState, type FormEvent } from "react";
import { authClient } from "../../lib/auth-client";
import { describeAuthError } from "../../lib/auth-errors";
import { parseInviteHash } from "../../lib/invite-link";
import { passwordProblem } from "../../lib/password";

type Invitation = { invite: string; email: string };
type Stage = { name: "reading" } | { name: "missing" } | { name: "ready"; invitation: Invitation } | { name: "sent"; email: string };

// The development server runs effects twice. The first run removes the token from the address bar, so the second would find nothing:
// keep what was read.
let captured: Invitation | null = null;

function readInvitation(): Invitation | null {
  const hash = window.location.hash;
  if (hash) {
    captured = parseInviteHash(hash);
    // Keep the token in memory only: take it out of the address bar and the browser's history.
    window.history.replaceState(null, "", window.location.pathname + window.location.search);
  }
  return captured;
}

export function SignUpForm() {
  const [stage, setStage] = useState<Stage>({ name: "reading" });
  const [name, setName] = useState("");
  const [password, setPassword] = useState("");
  const [pending, setPending] = useState(false);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    const invitation = readInvitation();
    setStage(invitation ? { name: "ready", invitation } : { name: "missing" });
  }, []);

  async function submit(event: FormEvent, invitation: Invitation) {
    event.preventDefault();
    const problem = passwordProblem(password);
    if (problem) return setError(problem);
    setPending(true);
    setError(null);
    const { error: failure } = await authClient.signUp.email({
      email: invitation.email,
      password,
      name: name.trim(),
      callbackURL: "/verify-email",
      fetchOptions: { headers: { "x-invite-token": invitation.invite } },
    });
    if (failure) {
      setError(describeAuthError(failure, "sign-up"));
      setPending(false);
      return;
    }
    setStage({ name: "sent", email: invitation.email });
  }

  if (stage.name === "reading") return <p className="lede" aria-live="polite">Reading your invitation</p>;

  if (stage.name === "missing") {
    return (
      <>
        <h1>You need an invitation</h1>
        <p className="lede">Accounts are by invitation. Open the link in the invitation email, or ask the person who runs this site to send you a new one.</p>
        <div>
          <Link className="btn" href="/sign-in">
            Go to sign in
          </Link>
        </div>
      </>
    );
  }

  if (stage.name === "sent") {
    return (
      <>
        <h1>Check your inbox</h1>
        <p className="lede">We sent a confirmation link to {stage.email}. Open it to finish creating your account.</p>
      </>
    );
  }

  const { invitation } = stage;
  return (
    <>
      <div className="stack">
        <h1>Create your account</h1>
        <p className="lede">You were invited to Page Chat. Choose a password to finish.</p>
      </div>
      <form className="form" onSubmit={(event) => submit(event, invitation)}>
        <label htmlFor="email">
          Invited address
          <input id="email" name="email" type="email" value={invitation.email} readOnly />
          <span className="hint">The invitation works only for this address.</span>
        </label>
        <label htmlFor="name">
          Your name
          <input id="name" name="name" type="text" autoComplete="name" required value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} />
        </label>
        <label htmlFor="password">
          Password
          <input id="password" name="password" type="password" autoComplete="new-password" required value={password} onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
          <span className="hint">At least 12 characters. A few ordinary words work well.</span>
        </label>
        {error ? (
          <div className="error-box" role="alert">
            {error}
          </div>
        ) : null}
        <div className="actions">
          <button className="btn btn-primary" type="submit" disabled={pending}>
            {pending ? "Creating account" : "Create account"}
          </button>
        </div>
      </form>
    </>
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 20: Create `web/src/app/sign-in/page.tsx`**

Independent reads run together: the session and the query string are awaited with `Promise.all`.

```
import type { Metadata } from "next";
import { redirect } from "next/navigation";
import { AuthShell } from "../../components/auth/AuthShell";
import { SignInForm } from "../../components/auth/SignInForm";
import { getSession } from "../../lib/session";

export const metadata: Metadata = { title: "Sign in" };

export default async function SignInPage({ searchParams }: { searchParams: Promise<{ deleted?: string; reset?: string }> }) {
  const [session, params] = await Promise.all([getSession(), searchParams]); // together: neither waits for the other
  if (session) redirect("/");
  return (
    <AuthShell>
      <SignInForm notice={params.deleted ? "deleted" : params.reset ? "reset" : null} />
    </AuthShell>
  );
}
```

```

import type { Metadata } from "next";
import Link from "next/link";
import { redirect } from "next/navigation";
import { AuthShell } from "../../components/auth/AuthShell";
import { ResendConfirmation } from "../../components/auth/ResendConfirmation";
import { getSession } from "../../lib/session";

export const metadata: Metadata = { title: "Confirm your email address" };

// The confirmation link in the email lands here. A good link has already signed the person in, so they go straight to the workspace.
export default async function VerifyEmailPage({ searchParams }: { searchParams: Promise<{ error?: string }> }) {
  const [session, params] = await Promise.all([getSession(), searchParams]);
  if (session && !params.error) redirect("/");
  if (params.error) {
    return (
      <AuthShell>
        <h1>This link has expired</h1>
        <p className="lede">Confirmation links work once and expire after an hour. Enter your address and we will send a new one.</p>
        <ResendConfirmation />
      </AuthShell>
    );
  }
  return (
    <AuthShell>
      <h1>Your address is confirmed</h1>
      <p className="lede">Sign in to carry on.</p>
      <div>
        <Link className="btn btn-primary" href="/sign-in">
          Sign in
        </Link>
      </div>
    </AuthShell>
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 27: Create `web/e2e/support/journeys.ts`**

Helpers that set a person up over HTTP, so a test does not click through screens another test already covers. Emailed links are read from the web app's log (the stack runs with `EMAIL_TRANSPORT=log`).

```
/**
 * Setting people up for a browser test without clicking through screens that another test already covers. Everything here goes over
 * HTTP to the real web app, and reads emailed links from its log (the stack runs with EMAIL_TRANSPORT=log).
 */
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import type { BrowserContext, Locator, Page } from "@playwright/test";
import { env } from "./env";

export const PASSWORD = "correct horse battery staple";
export const OWNER = "owner@example.com";
export type Member = { email: string; password: string; cookie: string };

let counter = 0;
export const uniqueEmail = (prefix: string) => `${prefix}-${Date.now().toString(36)}${counter++}@example.com`;

const sleep = (ms: number) => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
export const logLength = () => readFileSync(env.webLog, "utf8").length;

/** The first link in the newest email with this subject sent to `to`, written after `since` (a value from `logLength()`). */
export async function emailLink(to: string, subject: string, since = 0): Promise<string> {
  for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
    const log = readFileSync(env.webLog, "utf8").slice(since);
    const at = log.lastIndexOf(`[email to ${to}] ${subject}`);
    const link = at >= 0 ? /https?:\/\/\S+/.exec(log.slice(at))?.[0] : undefined;
    if (link) return link;
    await sleep(100);
  }
  throw new Error(`no "${subject}" email for ${to} appeared in the web server's log`);
}

const web = (path: string, init: RequestInit & { cookie?: string } = {}) =>
  fetch(`${env.web}${path}`, {
    ...init,
    redirect: "manual",
    headers: { "content-type": "application/json", origin: env.web, ...(init.cookie ? { cookie: init.cookie } : {}), ...(init.headers as Record<string, string>) },
  });

const cookieOf = (response: Response) => response.headers.getSetCookie().map((c) => c.split(";")[0]).join("; ");

export async function signInHttp(email: string, password = PASSWORD): Promise<string> {
  const response = await web("/api/auth/sign-in/email", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }) });
  if (response.status !== 200) throw new Error(`sign-in for ${email} answered ${response.status}`);
  return cookieOf(response);
}

async function confirm(email: string, since: number) {
  const link = await emailLink(email, "Confirm your email address", since);
  const response = await fetch(link, { redirect: "manual", headers: { origin: env.web } });
  if (![200, 302].includes(response.status)) throw new Error(`the confirmation link answered ${response.status}`);
}

/** The first administrator, ADMIN_EMAIL. Signs up once, and signs in on every later call. */
export async function ownerCookie(): Promise<string> {
  try {
    return await signInHttp(OWNER);
  } catch {
    const since = logLength();
    const signUp = await web("/api/auth/sign-up/email", { method: "POST", body: JSON.stringify({ email: OWNER, password: PASSWORD, name: "Owner" }) });
    if (signUp.status !== 200) throw new Error(`the owner's sign-up answered ${signUp.status}: ${await signUp.text()}`);
    await confirm(OWNER, since);
    return signInHttp(OWNER);
  }
}

/** Ask the administrator routes to invite `email`, and return the link from the email. */
export async function inviteLink(email: string): Promise<string> {
  const since = logLength();
  const response = await web("/api/admin/invites", { method: "POST", cookie: await ownerCookie(), body: JSON.stringify({ email }) });
  if (response.status !== 201) throw new Error(`inviting ${email} answered ${response.status}: ${await response.text()}`);
  return emailLink(email, "You are invited to Page Chat", since);
}

/** Someone with a confirmed account, ready to use. */
export async function createMember(prefix = "member"): Promise<Member> {
  const email = uniqueEmail(prefix);
  const invite = new URLSearchParams(new URL(await inviteLink(email)).hash.slice(1)).get("invite")!;
  const since = logLength();
  const signUp = await web("/api/auth/sign-up/email", {
    method: "POST",
    body: JSON.stringify({ email, password: PASSWORD, name: prefix }),
    headers: { "x-invite-token": invite },
  });
  if (signUp.status !== 200) throw new Error(`sign-up for ${email} answered ${signUp.status}: ${await signUp.text()}`);
  await confirm(email, since);
  return { email, password: PASSWORD, cookie: await signInHttp(email) };
}

/** Put a session cookie into the browser, so a test can start already signed in. */
export async function signInBrowser(context: BrowserContext, cookie: string): Promise<void> {
  await context.addCookies(
    cookie.split("; ").map((pair) => {
      const [name, ...value] = pair.split("=");
      return { name, value: value.join("="), url: env.web };
    }),
  );
}

/** An alert on the page that says `text`. (Next.js adds an alert of its own, empty, that announces page changes; ask for ours by what it says.) */
export const alertSaying = (page: Page, text: string): Locator => page.getByRole("alert").filter({ hasText: text });
```
